But it does kind of spill over. Most days my mind jumps from whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is about to quit, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal.\n\nIt’s like if there’s a small delay or someone doesn’t respond right away, my brain turns it into this whole bigger problem. I can usually keep functioning, but controlling it… I don’t know, it’s harder than I’d like to admit. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even outside of work stress?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a major way. I mean, I have days where I feel worn out or kind of flat, but I usually chalk that up to being tired or stressed. I wouldn’t say I feel hopeless, exactly.\n\nAs for interest in things… maybe I’m less enthusiastic sometimes, but I still care about things. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can't sit still, or find yourself pacing or fidgeting a lot when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I wouldn’t say I’m, like, bouncing off the walls or anything, but I do feel wound up a lot of the time. Even at home, when nothing is technically happening, it can feel like I’m still sitting in a pipeline review or waiting for some number to come in.\n\nAnd my shoulders are almost always tight. I try to relax them, but then a few minutes later I notice they’re right back up again. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling fatigued or drained most days, even if you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most days, no. I mean, I do get tired, but it usually feels like there’s a reason for it—work, not sleeping perfectly, just being busy. \n\nI don’t really notice feeling drained when I haven’t done much, if that makes sense. Or maybe I don’t pay enough attention to it. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience muscle tension, headaches, or other physical symptoms when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly muscle tension, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I do feel kind of wound up almost all the time. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even outside of work stress?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a major way. I mean, I have days where I feel worn out or kind of flat, but I usually chalk that up to being tired or stressed. I wouldn’t say I feel hopeless, exactly.\n\nAs for interest in things… maybe I’m less enthusiastic sometimes, but I still care about things. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can't sit still, or find yourself pacing or fidgeting a lot when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I wouldn’t say I’m, like, bouncing off the walls or anything, but I do feel wound up a lot of the time. Even at home, when nothing is technically happening, it can feel like I’m still sitting in a pipeline review or waiting for some number to come in.\n\nAnd my shoulders are almost always tight. I try to relax them, but then a few minutes later I notice they’re right back up again. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling fatigued or drained most days, even if you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most days, no. I mean, I do get tired, but it usually feels like there’s a reason for it—work, not sleeping perfectly, just being busy. \n\nI don’t really notice feeling drained when I haven’t done much, if that makes sense. Or maybe I don’t pay enough attention to it. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience muscle tension, headaches, or other physical symptoms when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly muscle tension, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I do feel kind of wound up almost all the time. Like even when I’m just at home eating dinner, part of me feels like I’m still sitting in a pipeline review waiting for numbers to come in.\n\nAnd my shoulders are probably the biggest thing. They stay tight, even when I notice it and try to drop them or relax. A few minutes later they’re right back up again. Is that what you mean by physical symptoms?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to fall asleep because your mind is racing with thoughts, even when you’re physically tired?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually kind of the main reason I came in, I think. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but my sleep has gotten really unreliable.\n\nEven when I’m exhausted and I get into bed, my brain just starts replaying forecasts or things going on with the team, like what I should’ve handled differently or what might go wrong. And then sometimes I wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning and I just… can’t get it to shut off again.\n\nI know everyone has work stress, so I’m not sure if that’s “enough” to count, but it’s been happening enough that it’s starting to worry me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up feeling unrefreshed, even after you’ve slept for a few hours?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not every single morning, but enough that I notice it.\n\nEven if I technically got a few hours, it doesn’t always feel like real sleep, if that makes sense. I’ll have been lying there with my mind going over forecasts or team stuff, and then if I wake up at 3 or 4, I can’t really get my brain to power down again. So by morning I’m up and functioning, but I don’t feel especially refreshed.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not sure if that’s… enough of a reason to come in, but it’s been wearing on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things even when you’re not at work, or having trouble controlling your anxiety about different aspects of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because some of it is just normal work pressure, I think. But it does kind of spill over. Most days my mind jumps from whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is about to quit, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal.\n\nIt’s like if there’s a small delay or someone doesn’t respond right away, my brain turns it into this whole bigger problem. I can usually keep functioning, but controlling it… I don’t know, it’s harder than I’d like to admit. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can't sit still, or find yourself pacing or fidgeting a lot when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I wouldn’t say I’m, like, bouncing off the walls or anything, but I do feel wound up a lot of the time. Even at home, when nothing is technically happening, it can feel like I’m still sitting in a pipeline review or waiting for some number to come in.\n\nAnd my shoulders are almost always tight. I try to relax them, but then a few minutes later I notice they’re right back up again. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling fatigued or drained most days, even if you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most days, no. I mean, I do get tired, but it usually feels like there’s a reason for it—work, not sleeping perfectly, just being busy. \n\nI don’t really notice feeling drained when I haven’t done much, if that makes sense. Or maybe I don’t pay enough attention to it. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience muscle tension, headaches, or other physical symptoms when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly muscle tension, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I do feel kind of wound up almost all the time. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but my sleep has gotten really unreliable.\n\nEven when I’m exhausted and I get into bed, my brain just starts replaying forecasts or things going on with the team, like what I should’ve handled differently or what might go wrong. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t really think it happens out of nowhere, if that’s what you mean. It’s usually that my brain grabs onto work stuff.

Like… the reason I’m here is my sleep has gotten pretty unreliable. Even when I’m exhausted, I’ll get into bed and start replaying forecasts or things going on with my team. And then if I wake up at 3 or 4, I can’t get my mind to power down again.

So I wouldn’t say it’s nothing specific, exactly. It feels tied to those thoughts. Is that helpful?

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thank you. I guess I’m here mostly because my sleep has been kind of unreliable lately. I mean, I can usually handle a lot with work, but even when I go to bed completely exhausted, I end up lying there replaying sales forecasts or issues with my team. And then I’ll wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning and it’s like my brain just won’t power down again. I’m not sure if that’s… enough of a reason to come in, but it’s been wearing on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things even when you’re not at work, or having trouble controlling your anxiety about different aspects of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because some of it is just normal work pressure, I think. But it does kind of spill over. Most days my mind jumps from whether we’ll hit the launch numbers, to whether someone on my team is about to quit, then suddenly I’m thinking about the mortgage or a client renewal.\n\nIt’s like if there’s a small delay or someone doesn’t respond right away, my brain turns it into this whole bigger problem. I can usually keep functioning, but controlling it… I don’t know, it’s harder than I’d like to admit. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, even outside of work stress?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a major way. I mean, I have days where I feel worn out or kind of flat, but I usually chalk that up to being tired or stressed. I wouldn’t say I feel hopeless, exactly.\n\nAs for interest in things… maybe I’m less enthusiastic sometimes, but I still care about things. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can't sit still, or find yourself pacing or fidgeting a lot when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I wouldn’t say I’m, like, bouncing off the walls or anything, but I do feel wound up a lot of the time. Even at home, when nothing is technically happening, it can feel like I’m still sitting in a pipeline review or waiting for some number to come in.\n\nAnd my shoulders are almost always tight. I try to relax them, but then a few minutes later I notice they’re right back up again. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling fatigued or drained most days, even if you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most days, no. I mean, I do get tired, but it usually feels like there’s a reason for it—work, not sleeping perfectly, just being busy. \n\nI don’t really notice feeling drained when I haven’t done much, if that makes sense. Or maybe I don’t pay enough attention to it. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience muscle tension, headaches, or other physical symptoms when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly muscle tension, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I do feel kind of wound up almost all the time. Like even when I’m just at home eating dinner, part of me feels like I’m still sitting in a pipeline review waiting for numbers to come in.\n\nAnd my shoulders are probably the biggest thing. They stay tight, even when I notice it and try to drop them or relax. A few minutes later they’re right back up again. Is that what you mean by physical symptoms?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to fall asleep because your mind is racing with thoughts, even when you’re physically tired?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually kind of the main reason I came in, I think. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but my sleep has gotten really unreliable.\n\nEven when I’m exhausted and I get into bed, my brain just starts replaying forecasts or things going on with the team, like what I should’ve handled differently or what might go wrong. And then sometimes I wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning and I just… can’t get it to shut off again.\n\nI know everyone has work stress, so I’m not sure if that’s “enough” to count, but it’s been happening enough that it’s starting to worry me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up feeling unrefreshed, even after you’ve slept for a few hours?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, not every single morning, but enough that I notice it.\n\nEven if I technically got a few hours, it doesn’t always feel like real sleep, if that makes sense. I’ll have been lying there with my mind going over forecasts or team stuff, and then if I wake up at 3 or 4, I can’t really get my brain to power down again. So by morning I’m up and functioning, but I don’t feel especially refreshed.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble sleeping that wasn't related to stress or work-related anxiety, like when things were going well in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… or at least, not in the same way. When things are going okay, I might have a random bad night like anyone, but this pattern feels really tied to work stress.\n\nThat’s kind of why I’m here, honestly. My sleep has gotten unreliable enough that even when I go to bed exhausted, I’m still lying there replaying forecasts or team issues, and then I’ll wake up at 3 or 4 and can’t get my brain to power down again.\n\nI’m not sure if that answers it correctly, but it doesn’t feel like it comes out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced difficulty falling asleep or staying asleep even during times when you weren’t feeling particularly stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had the occasional bad night when nothing major was going on, but the kind of sleep problem that made me come in seems pretty tied to feeling keyed up about work.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll be exhausted, get into bed, and then I’m still lying there replaying forecasts or team issues. And if I wake up around 3 or 4, I can’t get my brain to quiet down again. So I don’t think it happens in the same way when I’m not stressed… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble falling asleep or staying asleep on nights when you weren’t feeling stressed or anxious about work or anything else?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but the main issue seems to be when my brain is stuck on work.\n\nThat’s actually a big part of why I came in — my sleep has gotten unreliable enough that even if I go to bed exhausted, I’ll lie there replaying forecasts or things with my team. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can't sit still, or find yourself pacing or fidgeting a lot when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I wouldn’t say I’m, like, bouncing off the walls or anything, but I do feel wound up a lot of the time. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling fatigued or drained most days, even if you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most days, no. My sleep has gotten unreliable enough that even when I go to bed exhausted, I’m still lying there replaying forecasts or team issues, and then I’ll wake up at 3 or 4 and can’t get my brain to power down again.\n\nI’m not sure if that answers it correctly, but it doesn’t feel like it comes out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced difficulty falling asleep or staying asleep even during times when you weren’t feeling particularly stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had the occasional bad night when nothing major was going on, but the kind of sleep problem that made me come in seems pretty tied to feeling keyed up about work.\n\nIt’s more like I’ll be exhausted, get into bed, and then I’m still lying there replaying forecasts or team issues.
